{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-8.5359pt}{-3.93301pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}{}{}{{{}{}}}{}{}{{{}{}}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} {}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 11.81 0 L 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-4.84023pt}{6.79411pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{4.2095pt}{-10.87744pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{66}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\\
\triangleq H_{\ell}\left(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},\omega\right)\left(\sum_{\ell_{1}}W_{\ell,\ell_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})H_{\ell_{1}}\left(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},\omega\right)W_{\ell_{1},\ell_{0}}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})\right)H_{\ell_{0}}\left(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},\omega\right)(-\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{p}})\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{p}}H_{\ell_{0}}\left(-\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}},\omega\right)\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\omega+\omega_{0})\ .

(63)

After taking
t_{0}\to-\infty
and
\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}
, the only two contributions from the sum are
\ell_{1}=-1,1
, so that

\lim_{t_{0}\to-\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}D_{d}(\mathbf{r},\varphi,t;\mathbf{r}_{0},\varphi_{0},t_{0})=\frac{1}{2L^{4}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}w_{0}^{2}k_{\mathbf{n}}^{4}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}}(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}+r)^{-2}(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}+r)^{-1}

(64)

using

W_{0,\pm 1}(\mathbf{k})=-\frac{w_{0}}{2}[\mathring{\imath}k_{x}\pm k_{y}]\quad\text{ and }\quad W_{\pm 1,0}(\mathbf{k})=\frac{w_{0}}{2}[-\mathring{\imath}k_{x}\pm k_{y}]

(65)

from Eq. (
52a
),
so that
W_{0,1}(\mathbf{k})W_{1,0}(\mathbf{k})=W_{0,-1}(\mathbf{k})W_{-1,0}(\mathbf{k})=-w_{0}^{2}k^{2}/4
. After taking the limit
r\downarrow 0
in Eq. (
64
) the factor
k_{\mathbf{n}}^{4}
cancels with the same factor in
(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}+r)^{-2}
whenever
\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0}
, so that finally

\displaystyle\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})

\displaystyle=L^{-2}+\lim_{r\downarrow 0}\lim_{t_{0}\to-\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}D_{b}(\mathbf{r},\varphi,t;\mathbf{r}_{0},\varphi_{0},t_{0})+D_{d}(\mathbf{r},\varphi,t;\mathbf{r}_{0},\varphi_{0},t_{0})

(66a)

\displaystyle=L^{-2}-\frac{1}{L^{4}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0}}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}\frac{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}}}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\left\{1-\frac{w_{0}^{2}}{2\mathsf{D}_{t}}(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r})^{-1}\right\}+\mathcal{O}\!\left(w_{0}^{4}\right)+\ldots

(66b)

to be compared to the purely drift-diffusive Eq. (
162
)
and to
Eqs. (
44
) or (
46
),
derived using Mathieu functions, rather than the perturbative approach to self-propulsion here. By inspection of the two expressions, Eqs. (
44
) and (
66b
), the two are equivalent provided

1-\frac{w_{0}^{2}}{2\mathsf{D}_{t}}(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r})^{-1}+\mathcal{O}\!\left(w_{0}^{4}\right)=\mathsf{D}_{t}\Bigg\{\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}+w_{0}^{2}/(2\mathsf{D}_{r})+\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{4}\right)/k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}\big)^{-1}\\
+\frac{k_{\mathbf{n}}^{4}w_{0}^{2}}{2\mathsf{D}_{r}^{2}}\Big[\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}+w_{0}^{2}k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}/(2\mathsf{D}_{r})+\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{4}\right)\big)^{-1}-\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{2}\right)\big)^{-1}\Big]\Bigg\}

(67)

which can indeed be shown by expanding all terms on the right hand side to order
w_{0}^{2}
only, absorbing all other terms into
\mathcal{O}\!\left(w_{0}^{4}\right)
, for example

\mathsf{D}_{t}\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}+w_{0}^{2}/(2\mathsf{D}_{r})+\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{4}\right)/k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}\big)^{-1}=1-\frac{w_{0}^{2}}{2\mathsf{D}_{t}\mathsf{D}_{r}}+\mathcal{O}\!\left(w_{0}^{4}\right)\ .

(68)

This concludes the discussion of ABPs in two dimensions. In the following sections, we will calculate MSD and density in an external potential for ABPs in three dimensions, using the above as a template.

III
Active Brownian Particles in
3
dimensions

In three spatial dimensions, the diffusion of the director takes place on a curved manifold, which renders the Laplacian rather unwieldy, Eq. (
3
). The spherical harmonics provide a suitable eigensystem for this operator, but the self-propulsion term spoils the diagonalisation. As in the two-dimensional case, Section
II
, there are two possible ways ahead: Either find suitable special functions or deal with the self-propulsion term perturbatively. In the following subsection, we focus on the former.

III.1
”Three-dimensional Mathieu functions”

The harmonic part of the action is Eq. (
9
) with Eqs. (
2
) and (
3
) in Eq. (
5a
), more explicitly

\mathcal{A}_{01}[\tilde{\chi},\chi]=\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{3}x\,}\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}\sin\theta\,\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}t\,}{\!\mathrm{d}t\,}{\!\mathrm{d}t\,}{\!\mathrm{d}t\,}\tilde{\chi}(\mathbf{r},\theta,\varphi,t)\\
\bigg(-\partial_{t}+\mathsf{D}_{t}\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}^{2}-w_{0}(\sin\!\theta\cos\!\varphi\ \partial_{x}+\sin\!\theta\sin\!\varphi\ \partial_{y}+\cos\!\theta\ \partial_{z})+\mathsf{D}_{r}\big(\frac{1}{}\partial_{\theta}\sin\!\theta\ \partial_{\theta}+\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\!\theta}\partial_{\varphi}^{2}\big)-r\bigg)\chi(\mathbf{r},\theta,\varphi,t)

(69)

using the notation
\mathbf{r}=(x,y,z)^{\mathsf{T}}
.

The eigensystem that we will use now is modelled along the two-dimensional Mathieu functions, Eq. (
12
). Specifically, it is

\displaystyle\chi(\mathbf{r},\theta,\varphi,t)

\displaystyle=\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}\omega t}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}u_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta-\tau(\mathbf{k}),\varphi-\sigma(\mathbf{k}),\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k})\right)\chi_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

(70a)

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}(\mathbf{r},\theta,\varphi,t)

\displaystyle=\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}\omega t}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}\widetilde{u}_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta-\tau(-\mathbf{k}),\varphi-\sigma(-\mathbf{k}),\mathbf{q}(-\mathbf{k})\right)\tilde{\chi}_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

(70b)

with

\widetilde{u}_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k})\right)=\frac{1}{4\pi}u_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k})\right)

(71)

orthonormal, similar to Eq. (
14
)

\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}\sin\theta\,u_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta-\tau(\mathbf{k}),\varphi-\sigma(\mathbf{k}),\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k})\right)\widetilde{u}_{\ell^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}\left(\theta-\tau(-\mathbf{k}),\varphi-\sigma(-\mathbf{k}),\mathbf{q}(-\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\right)=\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}^{\prime})\delta_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}}

(72)

and

q(|\mathbf{k}|)
of the two-dimensional case, Eq. (
13
), replaced by

\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k})=\mathring{\imath}\frac{w_{0}\mathbf{k}}{\mathsf{D}_{r}}

(73)

and
two instead of one arbitrary functions
\sigma(\mathbf{k})
and
\tau(\mathbf{k})

cf.
Eq. (
14
).
The benefit of those, however, is rather limited compared to the two-dimensional case, where
\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{w}
was rewritten as
kw_{0}\cos(\varphi-\alpha)
. We therefore choose
\sigma\equiv\tau\equiv 0
and demand

\bigg(-\mathring{\imath}\frac{w_{0}}{\mathsf{D}_{r}}(k_{x}\sin\theta\cos\varphi+k_{y}\sin\theta\sin\varphi+k_{z}\cos\theta)+\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}+\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\theta}\partial_{\varphi}^{2}\bigg)u_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}\right)=-\lambda_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{q})u_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}\right)\ .

(74)

These eigenfunctions are characterised in some detail in App.
A
. For now, it suffices to know that they exist and that the eigenvalues
\lambda_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{q})
are indeed discrete and indexed in both
\ell
and
m
.
Having relegated the details of the eigensystem to the appendix, the bare propagator can now be determined without much ado,

\displaystyle\left\langle\chi_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{k},\omega)\tilde{\chi}_{\ell_{0}}^{m_{0}}(\mathbf{k}_{0},\omega_{0})\right\rangle_{0}=\frac{\delta_{\ell,\ell_{0}}\delta_{m,m_{0}}\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}_{0})\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\omega+\omega_{0})}{-\mathring{\imath}\omega+\mathsf{D}_{t}\mathbf{k}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k}))+r}=\delta_{\ell,\ell_{0}}\delta_{m,m_{0}}\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}_{0})\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\omega+\omega_{0})G_{\ell}^{m}\left(\mathbf{k},\omega\right)

(75a)

\displaystyle\triangleq\hbox to74.07pt{\vbox to32.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 34.79091pt\lower-16.1549pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {

}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}
}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}
}
\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\ ,

(75b)

and we proceed with the MSD.

III.1.1
Mean squared displacement

The MSD derives from the propagator Eq. (
75a
) via a double derivative. As in the two-dimensional case, the derivation simplifies considerably with uniform initialisation. Further, because the eigenequation (
74
) is particularly simple whenever
k_{x}=k_{y}=0
, we may write the MSD in terms of derivatives with respect to
k_{z}
only,

\overline{\mathbf{r}^{2}}(t)=-3\left.\partial_{k_{z}}^{2}\right|_{\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{0}}\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}\sin\theta\,\frac{1}{4\pi}\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\theta_{0}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta_{0}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta_{0}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta_{0}\,}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi_{0}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi_{0}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi_{0}\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi_{0}\,}\sin\theta_{0}\\
\times\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m}u_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}(k_{z}\mathbf{e}_{z})\right)\widetilde{u}_{\ell}^{m}\left(\varphi_{0},\theta_{0},\mathbf{q}(k_{z}\mathbf{e}_{z})\right)\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{q}(k_{z}\mathbf{e}_{z})))t}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{q}(k_{z}\mathbf{e}_{z})))t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{q}(k_{z}\mathbf{e}_{z})))t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{q}(k_{z}\mathbf{e}_{z})))t\right)}\ .

(76)

Similar to Eq. (
22
), we use coefficients

\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}\sin\theta\,u_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,q\mathbf{e}_{z}\right)=4\pi A_{\ell,0}^{0,0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})\delta_{m,0}

(77)

as derived in more detail in Eq. (
138
). These coefficients we need to determine at most up to order
q^{2}
in order to express
\overline{\mathbf{r}^{2}}(t)
in closed form. Using the notation
q=\mathring{\imath}w_{0}k_{z}/\mathsf{D}_{r}
in

\mathbf{q}(k_{z}\mathbf{e}_{z})=q\mathbf{e}_{z}
, Eq. (
76
) produces with Eq. (
77
)

\overline{\mathbf{r}^{2}}(t)=-3\left.\partial_{k_{z}}^{2}\right|_{\mathbf{k}=\mathbf{0}}\left\{\left(1-\frac{q^{2}}{24}\right)^{2}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{z}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{0}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z}))t}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{z}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{0}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z}))t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{z}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{0}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z}))t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{z}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{0}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z}))t\right)}+\left(\frac{q}{2\sqrt{3}}\right)^{2}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{z}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{1}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{z}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{1}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{z}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{1}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{z}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\lambda_{1}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})\right)}\right\}

(78)

from the expansion of

A_{0,0}^{0,0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})
, Eq. (
133a
),
and

A_{1,0}^{0,0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})
, Eq. (
134a
),
with

A_{\ell,0}^{0,0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})\in\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{2}\right)
for
\ell\geq 2
, Eq. (
135
).
The two eigenvalues
\lambda_{0}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})=-q^{2}/6+\ldots
and
\lambda_{1}^{0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})=2+\ldots
, Eqs. (
129
) and (
131
), need to be known only to leading order in
q
. Performing the derivative and re-arranging terms then produces the final result

\overline{\mathbf{r}^{2}}(t)=6\mathsf{D}_{t}t+\frac{w_{0}^{2}}{2\mathsf{D}_{r}^{2}}\left(\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-2\mathsf{D}_{r}t}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-2\mathsf{D}_{r}t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-2\mathsf{D}_{r}t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-2\mathsf{D}_{r}t\right)}-1+2\mathsf{D}_{r}t\right)

(79)

structurally similar to the result in two dimensions, Eq. (
26
), and indeed identical to the MSD of Run-and-Tumble particles in three dimensions, Eq. (49) of
ZhangPruessner:2022
, if the tumble rate
\alpha
is replaced by
2\mathsf{D}_{r}

Cates:2013
;
Cates:2015
. The effective diffusion constant in three dimensions is

\mathsf{D}^{\text{3D}}_{\text{eff}}=\mathsf{D}_{t}+\frac{w_{0}^{2}}{6\mathsf{D}_{r}}\ ,

(80)

to be compared to Eq. (
27
).

III.1.2
External Potential

Allowing for an external potential and confining the particles to a finite space means that the fields are now written as

\displaystyle\chi(\mathbf{r},\theta,\varphi,t)

\displaystyle=\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}\frac{1}{L^{3}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in\mathds{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}\omega t}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}u_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k})\right)\chi_{\ell}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

(81a)

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}(\mathbf{r},\theta,\varphi,t)

\displaystyle=\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}\frac{1}{L^{3}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in\mathds{Z}^{3}}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}\omega t}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}\widetilde{u}_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}(-\mathbf{k})\right)\tilde{\chi}_{\ell}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

(81b)

similar to Eqs. (
28
) and (
70
)
and any expectation needs to be taken with the perturbative action

\mathcal{A}_{P1}=\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}\frac{1}{L^{9}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{1},\mathbf{n}_{2},\mathbf{n}_{3}}L^{3}\delta_{\mathbf{n}_{1}+\mathbf{n}_{2}+\mathbf{n}_{3},\mathbf{0}}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{3}}\sum_{m_{1},m_{3}}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{2}})\tilde{\chi}_{\ell_{1},m_{1}}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}},-\omega)\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}_{2}}\chi_{\ell_{3},m_{3}}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{3}},\omega)\Delta_{\ell_{1},\ell_{3}}^{m_{1},m_{3}}(-\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}},-\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{3}})\ ,

(82)

similar to Eq. (
32
)
with integral

\Delta_{\ell_{1},\ell_{3}}^{m_{1},m_{3}}(-\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}},-\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{3}})=\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}\sin\theta\,u_{\ell_{3}}^{m_{3}}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{3}})\right)\widetilde{u}_{\ell_{1}}^{m_{1}}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{1}})\right)\ ,

(83)

similar to Eq. (
31
).

The resulting diagrammatics of the full propagator to leading order in the potential is

{}{}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 L -3.94 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}}{{}}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin}
{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.6}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.6}{0.35455pt}{0.0pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.77 0 L -1.66 2.21 L 0 0 L -1.66 -2.21}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }\lxSVG@closescope }{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}{}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{{{}{}{{}}
}}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{{}}
}
\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope }
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}+\ldots\ ,

(84)

as in Eq. (
35
) but
with the index
\ell
replaced by the couple
\ell,m
.
The arguments that follow to extract the stationary density are similar to those in Section
II.1.2
. Yet, to be able to draw on the results in App.
A
, all
\mathbf{k}
-vectors featuring in the three-dimensional Mathieu functions need to be parallel to
\mathbf{e}_{z}
. Because amputated diagrams draw all
\mathbf{k}
-dependence from spatial variation of the potential, we thus demand
\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})=\Upsilon(z)
,
i.e.
that the potential depends only on the
z
-component of
\mathbf{r}=(x,y,z)^{\mathsf{T}}
. Its Fourier transform can therefore be written in the form

\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}}=\iiint\limits_{0}^{\hskip 8.19447ptL}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{3}r\,}\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}=L^{2}\delta_{n_{x},0}\delta_{n_{y},0}\Upsilon_{n_{z}}

(85)

where
\mathbf{n}=(n_{x},n_{y},n_{z})^{\mathsf{T}}
. The notation of Eq. (
85
) is slightly ambigious,
as
\Upsilon_{n_{z}}
on the right is a Fourier transform in the
z
-direction only, while
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}}
on the left is a Fourier transform in all space, yet the alternatives seem to obfuscate even more.

Just like in
Section
II.1.2
, the stationary limit enforces
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}}=\mathbf{0}
, as well as
\ell_{0}=0
, as otherwise
\lambda_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{q}(0))\neq 0
, Eq. (
116b
), in the propagator Eq. (
75a
).

Turning our attention to the projection
\Delta_{\ell_{1},0}^{m_{1},m_{3}}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}})
for
\mathbf{p}=\mathbf{0}
, this vanishes whenever
m_{3}\neq 0
as
u_{\ell=0}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}=\mathbf{0}\right)=\sqrt{4\pi}Y_{0}^{m}(\theta,\varphi)=\delta_{m,0}
, Eq. (
117
),
because the spherical harmonics that the three-dimensional Mathieu functions are written in with
\ell=0
allow only for
m=0
, so that
Y_{0}^{m}(\theta,\varphi)=\delta_{m,0}/\sqrt{4\pi}
. It follows that

\Delta_{\ell,0}^{m,m_{0}}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},\mathbf{0})=\delta_{m_{0},0}\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}\sin\theta\,\widetilde{u}_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})\right)=\delta_{m_{0},0}\delta_{m,0}A_{\ell,0}^{0,0}(\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}))\ ,

(86)

for all
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\parallel\mathbf{e}_{z}
. Eq. (
86
) corresponds to Eq. (
39
). This produces

\lim_{t_{0}\to-\infty}\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},\theta,\varphi,t)\tilde{\chi}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}},\theta_{0},\varphi_{0},t_{0})\right\rangle=u_{0}^{0}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{0}\right)\widetilde{u}_{0}^{0}\left(\theta_{0},\varphi_{0},\mathbf{0}\right)L^{2}\delta_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{p},\mathbf{0}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{0}}\\
+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m}(-\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{p}})\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{p}}G_{\ell}^{m}\left(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},0\right)\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{0}}\delta_{m,0}A_{\ell,0}^{0,0}(\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}))u_{\ell}^{m}\left(\theta,\varphi,\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})\right)\widetilde{u}_{0}^{0}\left(\theta_{0},\varphi_{0},\mathbf{0}\right)+\ldots\ .

(87)

similar to Eq. (
40
), with
G_{\ell}^{m}\left(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},0\right)
denoting the bare propagator Eq. (
75a
) and again assuming
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\parallel\mathbf{e}_{z}
. This is guaranteed by Eq. (
85
), which means that
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}}
vanishes for all
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\nparallel\mathbf{e}_{z}
, so that the second term in Eq. (
87
) only ever contributes when
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\parallel\mathbf{e}_{z}
.

Since
\widetilde{u}_{0}^{0}\left(\theta_{0},\varphi_{0},\mathbf{0}\right)=1/(4\pi)
, it cancels the
4\pi
of the integral over the final angles
\theta
and
\varphi
of both terms on the right hand side of Eq. (
87
), the second one by Eq. (
77
),

\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}\sin\theta\,\lim_{t_{0}\to-\infty}\left\langle\chi(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},\theta,\varphi,t)\tilde{\chi}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}},\theta_{0},\varphi_{0},t_{0})\right\rangle\\
=L^{3}\delta_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{p},\mathbf{0}}\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{0}}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}(-\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{p}})\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}+\mathbf{p}}G_{\ell}^{m}\left(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}},0\right)\delta_{\mathbf{p},\mathbf{0}}\delta_{m,0}\big(A_{\ell,0}^{0,0}(\mathbf{q}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}))\big)^{2}+\ldots\ ,

(88)

similar to Eq. (
41
). Expanding to order
q^{2}=-w_{0}^{2}k_{z}^{2}/\mathsf{D}_{r}^{2}
, Eq. (
73
), means to keep only
\ell=0,1
in the sum in Eq. (
88
), as
A_{\ell,0}^{0,0}(q\mathbf{e}_{z})\in\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{\ell}\right)
, Eq. (
135
). This produces an expression similar to Eq. (
42
), and using Eq. (
133a
) and (
134a
) for
A_{\ell,0}^{0,0}
, as well as Eqs. (
129
) and (
131
) for
\lambda_{\ell}^{0}
, finally gives

\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})=L^{-3}-\frac{1}{L^{4}}\sum_{n_{z}\neq 0}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}zk_{n_{z}}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}zk_{n_{z}}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}zk_{n_{z}}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}zk_{n_{z}}\right)}\Upsilon_{n_{z}}\Bigg\{\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}+w_{0}^{2}/(6\mathsf{D}_{r})+\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{4}\right)/k_{n_{z}}^{2}\big)^{-1}+\frac{k_{n_{z}}^{4}w_{0}^{2}}{12\mathsf{D}_{r}^{2}}\Big[\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{n_{z}}^{2}+w_{0}^{2}k_{n_{z}}^{2}/(6\mathsf{D}_{r})+\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{4}\right)\big)^{-1}\\
-\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{n_{z}}^{2}+2\mathsf{D}_{r}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{2}\right)\big)^{-1}\Big]\Bigg\}+\mathcal{O}\!\left(q^{4}\right)+\ldots\ ,

(89)

using
\mathbf{r}=(x,y,z)^{\mathsf{T}}
for the position,

k_{n_{z}}=2\pi n_{z}/L
for the mode, and Eq. (
85
), which cancels
L^{2}
.
Eq. (
89
) corresponds to Eq. (
44
) in two dimension.
Expanding rather in small
k_{n_{z}}
, similar to Eq. (
46
), produces

\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})=L^{-3}-\frac{1}{L^{4}}\sum_{n_{z}\neq 0}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}zk_{n_{z}}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}zk_{n_{z}}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}zk_{n_{z}}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}zk_{n_{z}}\right)}\frac{\Upsilon_{n_{z}}}{\mathsf{D}^{\text{3D}}_{\text{eff}}}\left\{1+\frac{w_{0}^{2}k_{n_{z}}^{2}}{12\mathsf{D}_{r}^{2}}\bigg(1-\frac{11}{90}\frac{w_{0}^{2}}{\mathsf{D}_{r}\mathsf{D}^{\text{3D}}_{\text{eff}}}\bigg)+\mathcal{O}\!\left(k_{\mathbf{n}}^{4}\right)\right\}+\mathcal{O}\!\left(\Upsilon^{2}\right)

(90)

which has non-trivial corrections compared to the purely drift-diffusive Eq. (
162
), beyond setting
in the latter
\mathbf{w}_{0}=\mathbf{0}
and replacing the diffusion constant

\mathsf{D}_{t}
by
\mathsf{D}^{\text{3D}}_{\text{eff}}
of Eq. (
80
). The difference in
L^{-4}
compared to
L^{-2d}
in Eq. (
162
) is solely due to the definition of
\Upsilon_{n_{z}}
in Eq. (
85
).

This concludes the calculation of properties of three-dimensional active Brownian particles on the basis of ”three-dimensional Mathieu functions”. In the next two sections, we will reproduce the results above on the basis of a perturbation theory in the drift.

III.2
Perturbation in small
w_{0}

Along the lines of Section
II.2
, we return now to calculate the MSD and the stationary density while treating the self-propulsion as a perturbation over diffusion, so that we can use the eigensystem

\displaystyle\chi(\mathbf{r},\theta,\varphi,t)

\displaystyle=\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=-\ell}^{\ell}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}\omega t}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}(4\pi)^{1/2}Y_{\ell}^{m}(\theta,\varphi)\chi_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

(91a)

\displaystyle\tilde{\chi}(\mathbf{r},\theta,\varphi,t)

\displaystyle=\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22\omega\,}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=-\ell}^{\ell}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}\omega t}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\omega t\right)}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}(4\pi)^{-1/2}\Big(Y_{\ell}^{m}(\theta,\varphi)\Big)^{*}\tilde{\chi}_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{k},\omega)\ .

(91b)

where

Y_{\ell}^{m}(\theta,\varphi)=\sqrt{\frac{(2\ell+1)}{4\pi}\frac{(\ell-m)!}{(\ell+m)!}}P_{\ell}^{m}(\cos\!\theta)\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}m\varphi}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}m\varphi\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}m\varphi\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}m\varphi\right)}

(92)

denote the well-known spherical harmonics with
P_{\ell}^{m}
denotes the standard associated Legendre polynomials which carry a factor
(-1)^{m}
in their definition, so that, for example
Y_{1}^{1}(\theta,\varphi)=-\frac{1}{2}\sqrt{\frac{3}{2\pi}}\sin\!\theta\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\varphi}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\varphi\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\varphi\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\varphi\right)}
. Our definition of
Y_{\ell}^{m}
conforms to
SphericalHarmonicsY[
\ell,m,\theta,\varphi
]

Mathematica:10.0.2.0
. The spherical harmonics
Y_{\ell}^{m}
are orthonormal

\int_{0}^{\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\theta\,}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\varphi\,}\sin\theta\,Y_{\ell}^{m}(\theta,\varphi)\Big(Y_{\ell^{\prime}}^{m^{\prime}}(\theta,\varphi)\Big)^{*}=\delta_{\ell,\ell^{\prime}}\delta_{m,m^{\prime}}

(93)

and obey the eigenvalue equation

\nabla^{2}_{\Omega}Y_{\ell}^{m}(\theta,\varphi)=\left(\frac{1}{\sin\!\theta}\partial_{\theta}\sin\!\theta\ \partial_{\theta}+\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\!\theta}\partial_{\varphi}^{2}\right)Y_{\ell}^{m}(\theta,\varphi)=-\ell(\ell+1)Y_{\ell}^{m}(\theta,\varphi)\ .

(94)

The
harmonic part of the action Eq. (
10a
) now reads

\mathcal{A}_{02}[\tilde{\chi},\chi]=-\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\omega\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\omega\,}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}{\!\mathrm{d}\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22^{3}k\,}\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\sum_{m=-\ell}^{\ell}\tilde{\chi}_{\ell}^{m}(-\mathbf{k},-\omega)(-\mathring{\imath}\omega+\mathsf{D}_{t}k^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\ell(\ell+1)+r)\chi_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{k},\omega)

(95)

so that the bare propagators are

\left\langle\chi_{\ell}^{m}(\mathbf{k},\omega)\tilde{\chi}_{\ell_{0}}^{m_{0}}(\mathbf{k}_{0},\omega_{0})\right\rangle_{0}=\frac{\delta_{\ell,\ell_{0}}\delta_{m,m_{0}}\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}_{0})\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\omega+\omega_{0})}{-\mathring{\imath}\omega+\mathsf{D}_{t}\mathbf{k}^{2}+\mathsf{D}_{r}\ell(\ell+1)+r}=\delta_{\ell,\ell_{0}}\delta_{m,m_{0}}\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\mathbf{k}+\mathbf{k}_{0})\delta\mkern-6.0mu\mathchar 22(\omega+\omega_{0})H_{\ell}^{m}\left(\mathbf{k},\omega\right)\\
\triangleq\hbox to74.07pt{\vbox to32.31pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 34.79091pt\lower-16.1549pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {
