\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}_{\text{$i+1$ baubles }}

(163c)

using Eq. (
148
). In one dimension we can further simplify,

E_{j+1}(k_{n})=-\frac{\mathcal{I}(n\neq 0)}{\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{n}+\mathring{\imath}w_{0}}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{q\in\mathds{Z}}\Upsilon_{n-q}k_{n-q}E_{j}(k_{q})\ .

(164)

From Eq. (
163
) it can be gleaned that in real-space
E_{j}(\mathbf{r})
obeys the recurrence

\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}^{2}-\mathbf{w}\cdot\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\big)E_{j+1}(\mathbf{r})=-\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\big(E_{j}(\mathbf{r})\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})\big)\ ,

(165)

where we have refrained from introducing a separate symbol for the inverse Fourier transform
E_{j}(\mathbf{r})
of
E_{j}(\mathbf{k})
.
It is instructive to demonstrate that this implies that Eq. (
157
)
therefore solves the Fokker-Planck Eq. (
145
) at stationarity,
i.e.
we want to show that using Eq. (
165
) in Eq. (
157
) provides a solution of Eq. (
145
) with
\partial_{t}\rho=0
,

0=\mathsf{D}_{t}\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}^{2}\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})-\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\cdot\big(\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})(\mathbf{w}-\nabla\Upsilon(\mathbf{r}))\big)\ .

(166)

To see this, we sum both sides of Eq. (
165
) over
j=0\ldots
, which on the left-hand side produces
\rho_{0}
of Eq. (
157
), including the vanishing term

\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}^{2}-\mathbf{w}\cdot\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\big)E_{0}(\mathbf{r})=0

(167)

as
E_{0}(\mathbf{r})=L^{-d}
, Eq. (
158
), so that Eq. (
165
) implies

\big(\mathsf{D}_{t}\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}^{2}-\mathbf{w}\cdot\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\big)\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}E_{j}(\mathbf{r})=-\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\big(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}E_{j}(\mathbf{r})\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})\big)\ ,

(168)

and thus Eq. (
166
), with the
E_{j}
in Eq. (
157
) determined by Eq. (
163
).

Eq. (
163
) is the central result of the present section.
By means of Eq. (
157
), it allows the calculation of
the stationary density starting from
E_{0}
, Eq. (
158
). Without
drift, the density should produce the Boltzmann factor, which we will
recover in the next section. While this result is easy to obtain from a
differential equation approach in real-space
\mathbf{r}
, it is very
instructive to perform this calculation in
\mathbf{k}
-space. With drift, on
the other hand, we can compare the density
\rho_{0}
resulting from
Eqs. (
163
) to the known stationary density
in one dimension
[
22
]
, which is done in Section
C.5
.

C.4
Barometric formula

In the absence of a drift,
\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{0}
, the density will evolve into the Boltzmann
factor

\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})=\frac{1}{\mathcal{N}}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}

(169)

with normalisation constant

\mathcal{N}=\int\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}^{d}r\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{d}r\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{d}r\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{d}r\,}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}\ .

(170)

Eq. (
169
) is the stationary solution of the Fokker-Planck Eq. (
145
) without drift,

0=\mathsf{D}_{t}\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}^{2}\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})+\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\big(\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})\nabla_{\mathbf{r}}\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})\big)\ ,

(171)

which demands that the probability current is spatially uniform

[
23
]
.
Provided the potential is bounded from above and from below and with
boundary conditions given, the solution is unique
by Eq. (
169
), including the normalisation constant given by
Eq. (
170
).

In the following, we show the equivalence of Eq. (
169
) and
the field-theoretic result Eqs. (
157
), (
158
)
and (
163
), by expanding the density
\rho_{0}
from Eq. (
169
) in terms of some
B_{j}
, to be characterised next.

C.4.1
Properties of
B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})

We expand
the exponential in Eq. (
169
) order by order in the potential

\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})=\mathcal{N}^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{j!}\left(-\frac{\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{j}

(172)

and introduce Fourier-modes

B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=\int_{0}^{L}\ldots\int_{0}^{L}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}^{d}r\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{d}r\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{d}r\,}{\!\mathrm{d}^{d}r\,}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}\frac{1}{j!}\left(-\frac{\Upsilon(\mathbf{r})}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{j}

(173)

so that

\rho_{0}(\mathbf{r})=\mathcal{N}^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{L^{d}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}\in\mathds{Z}}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{r}\right)}B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})\ ,

(174)

similar to Eq. (
157
).
The normalisation Eq. (
170
) can therefore be written as a sum over the
\mathbf{0}
-modes of
B_{j}
in Eq. (
173
)

\mathcal{N}=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}B_{j}(\mathbf{0})\ .

(175)

That
E_{j}(\mathbf{r})
as used in Eq. (
157
) and defined in Eq. (
156
) solves Eq. (
171
) has been established in Eq. (
168
) via the recurrence Eq. (
163
). By construction, the
E_{j}
, in Eq. (
157
) provide an expansion of the density order by order in the potential
\Upsilon
.
The
B_{j}
, Eq. (
174
), on the other hand, are an expansion in orders of the potential of only the exponential, lacking, however, the normalisation.
Both
E_{j}
and
B_{j}
are, by construction, of order
j
in the potential
\Upsilon
.
While generally
E_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})\neq B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})/\mathcal{N}
, we will show in the following that

\rho_{0}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}E_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=\frac{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})}{B_{0}(\mathbf{0})+B_{1}(\mathbf{0})+\ldots}\ ,

(176)

taking the Fourier transform on both sides of Eqs. (
157
) and (
174
), and using Eq. (
175
).
Equivalently, we show that

B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})

is
the sum of the terms of
j
th order in
\Upsilon
of
\mathcal{N}\sum_{j}E_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})
,

\sum_{i=0}^{j}B_{i}(\mathbf{0})E_{j-i}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})\ .

(177)

We will thus show, beyond Eq. (
168
), that the
E_{j}
are correctly normalised, providing us with an expansion of the density
\rho_{0}
order by order in the potential.
Showing this, will further provide us with a useful, non-trivial algebraic identity.

To proceed, we firstly confirm Eq. (
177
) for
j=0,1
by determining
B_{j}(\mathbf{k})
directly from Eq. (
173
)

\displaystyle B_{0}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})

\displaystyle=L^{d}\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}

(178a)

\displaystyle B_{1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})

\displaystyle=-\frac{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}}}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}

(178b)

so that Eq. (
158
) indeed confirms

B_{0}(\mathbf{0})E_{0}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=L^{d}\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}=B_{0}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})

(179)

and Eqs. (
158
) and (
159
) with

\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{0}
confirm

B_{0}(\mathbf{0})E_{1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})+B_{1}(\mathbf{0})E_{0}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=-L^{d}\frac{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}}\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\frac{1}{L^{d}}-\frac{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{0}}}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}=-\frac{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}}}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\big(\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})+\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}\big)=B_{1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})

(180)

as
\Upsilon_{\mathbf{0}}\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}=\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}}\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}
and

1=\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})+\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}\ .

(181)

To characterise
B_{j}
more generally, we
introduce the recursion formula

B_{j+1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=-\frac{1}{(j+1)\mathsf{D}_{t}}\frac{1}{L^{d}}\sum_{\mathbf{q}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{q}}B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{q}})

(182)

by elementary calculations on the basis of Eq. (
173
) and similar to Eq. (
163
). Starting from Eq. (
178a
), it can be used to produce for
j\geq 2

B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=\frac{L^{d}}{j!}\left(\frac{-1}{L^{d}\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{j}\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{j-1},\ldots,\mathbf{n}_{1}}\underbrace{\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j-1}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}_{j-1}-\mathbf{n}_{j-2}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}_{2}-\mathbf{n}_{1}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}}_{\text{$j$ factors of $\Upsilon$}}\ .

(183)

This expression
can be simplified further by substituting dummy variables according to
\mathbf{p}_{i}=\mathbf{n}_{i}-\mathbf{n}_{i-1}
for
i=j-1,j-2,\ldots,1
with the convention
\mathbf{n}_{0}=\mathbf{0}
. As for

\mathbf{p}_{j}=\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j-1}=\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{p}_{j-1}-\mathbf{n}_{j-2}=\ldots=\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{p}_{j-1}-\mathbf{p}_{j-2}-\ldots-\mathbf{p}_{1}\ ,

(184)

this can be allowed to run in the sum by enforcing Eq. (
184
) via a Kronecker
\delta
-function, so that eventually

B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=\frac{L^{d}}{j!}\left(\frac{-1}{L^{d}\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{j}\sum_{\mathbf{p}_{j},\ldots,\mathbf{p}_{1}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j-1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{1}}\delta_{\mathbf{p}_{1}+\ldots+\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\ .

(185)

Even when calculated with a convolution in mind, Eq. (
185
) is valid for
j=1
by direct comparison with Eq. (
178b
) and, by comparison with Eq. (
178a
), even
j=0
with the convention that the final
\delta_{\mathbf{p}_{1}+\ldots+\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{n}}
degenerates into
\delta_{\mathbf{0},\mathbf{n}}
for
j=0
.

In the form Eq. (
185
), we can show easily an important identity, based on the observation

\sum_{\mathbf{p}_{j},\ldots,\mathbf{p}_{1}}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j-1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{1}}\delta_{\mathbf{p}_{1}+\ldots\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{n}}=\sum_{\mathbf{p}_{j},\ldots,\mathbf{p}_{1}}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{i}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j-1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{1}}\delta_{\mathbf{p}_{1}+\ldots\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{n}}

(186)

for all
i\in\{1,2,\ldots,j\}
, which labels the index of
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{i}}
on the right-hand side as the
\mathbf{p}_{i}
are all equivalent dummy variables. Summing this sum for
i=1,2,\ldots,j
therefore gives
j
times the same result, while the sum of the
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{i}}
gives
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{1}}+\ldots+\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}=\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}
inside the sum, according to the Kronecker
\delta
-function. In conclusion for
j\geq 1

j\sum_{\mathbf{p}_{j},\ldots,\mathbf{p}_{1}}\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j-1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{1}}\delta_{\mathbf{p}_{1}+\ldots\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{n}}=\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\sum_{\mathbf{p}_{j},\ldots,\mathbf{p}_{1}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j-1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{1}}\delta_{\mathbf{p}_{1}+\ldots\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\ ,

(187)

and thus

\sum_{\mathbf{p}_{j},\ldots,\mathbf{p}_{1}}\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})\frac{\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}}{k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j-1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{1}}\delta_{\mathbf{p}_{1}+\ldots\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{n}}=\frac{1}{j}\sum_{\mathbf{p}_{j},\ldots,\mathbf{p}_{1}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j-1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{1}}\delta_{\mathbf{p}_{1}+\ldots\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{n}}\ ,

(188)

where the indicator function on the left avoids the divergence of
\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}/k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}
.
Using Eq. (
188
) in Eq. (
185
) gives

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})\frac{L^{d}}{(j-1)!}\left(\frac{-1}{L^{d}\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{j}\sum_{\mathbf{p}_{j},\ldots,\mathbf{p}_{1}}\frac{\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}}{k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{j-1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{p}_{1}}\delta_{\mathbf{p}_{1}+\ldots\mathbf{p}_{j},\mathbf{n}}

(189a)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})\frac{L^{d}}{(j-1)!}\left(\frac{-1}{L^{d}\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{j}\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{j-1},\ldots,\mathbf{n}_{1}}\frac{\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j-1}}}{k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j-1}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}_{j-1}-\mathbf{n}_{j-2}}\ldots\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}_{2}-\mathbf{n}_{1}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}_{1}}

(189b)

\displaystyle=-\frac{\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\frac{1}{L^{d}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{j-1}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}}\frac{\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j-1}}}{k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j-1}}B_{j-1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{j-1}})

(189c)

where the second equality is a matter of labelling the indices again in
\mathbf{n}
and
\mathbf{n}_{1\ldots j-1}
using Eq. (
184
) in reverse, and thus reverting from the indexing in Eq. (
185
) to that in Eq. (
183
), which does not carry a Kronecker
\delta
-function.
In this form the summation can again be written recursively, similarly to Eq. (
182
), which is done after the third equality, by absorbing the summation over
\mathbf{n}_{j-2},\ldots,\mathbf{n}_{1}
into
B_{j-1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{j-1}})
.
Eq. (
189c
) is the key outcome of the present section.

C.4.2
Relationship between
E_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})
and
B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})

We proceed with confirming
Eq. (
177
)
by induction, using Eq. (
189c
) in the course. To access this identity, we firstly rewrite Eq. (
177
) using Eq. (
181
), so that for
j\geq 0

B_{j+1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=B_{j+1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})(\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})+\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}})=-\frac{\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\frac{1}{L^{d}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{j}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}}\frac{\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j}}}{k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j}}B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{j}})+B_{j+1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}\ .

(190)

Of the two terms on the right hand side, the first can be rewritten using Eq. (
177
), which is
assumed
to hold for
j
. This allows us to replace
B_{j}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{j}})
by a sum.
In the second term we replace
\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}
by
E_{0}(\mathbf{k}_{n})
according to Eq. (
158
), resulting in

B_{j+1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=-\frac{\mathcal{I}(\mathbf{n}\neq\mathbf{0})}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\frac{1}{L^{d}}\sum_{\mathbf{n}_{j}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}}\frac{\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}}\cdot\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j}}}{k_{\mathbf{n}}^{2}}\Upsilon_{\mathbf{n}-\mathbf{n}_{j}}\sum_{i=0}^{j}B_{i}(\mathbf{0})E_{j-i}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}_{j}})+B_{j+1}(\mathbf{0})E_{0}(\mathbf{k}_{n})\ .

(191)

The summation over
\mathbf{n}_{j}\in\mathds{Z}^{d}
can now be performed on
E_{j-i}
using Eq. (
163
) at
\mathbf{w}=\mathbf{0}
,

B_{j+1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})=\sum_{i=0}^{j}B_{i}(\mathbf{0})E_{j-i+1}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})+B_{j+1}(\mathbf{0})E_{0}(\mathbf{k}_{n})=\sum_{i=0}^{j+1}B_{i}(\mathbf{0})E_{j+1-i}(\mathbf{k}_{\mathbf{n}})\quad\text{ for }\ j\geq 0\ ,

(192)

completing our demonstration that Eq. (
177
) holds for
j+1
if it holds for
j
. With the induction basis Eqs. (
179
) or (
180
) this concludes our demonstration that the
diagrams Eq. (
156
) or equivalently
(
163
)
produce a normalised expansion of the density
\rho_{0}

(
157
) identical to the Boltzmann factor, order by order in the external potential. The field-theoretic approach thus determines the stationary density without the need of an
a posteriori
normalisation. And while the field theory is an expansion necessarily in a sufficiently weak potential and necessarily in a finite volume, no such restriction applies to the resulting Boltzmann factor.

In the next section we will similarly demonstrate the correspondence between classical and field-theoretic stationary density in the presence of a drift
w
.

C.5
Density in one dimension with drift

In this section, we will demonstrate that the expansion Eq. (
157
) in terms of diagrams Eq. (
156
) produces the correct, normalised, stationary result in the presence of a potential and a drift in one dimension. The restriction to one dimension is because we are not aware of a closed-form expression beyond.

Following the pattern in Section
C.4
, we want to show that the diagrammatic expansion Eq. (
157
) order by order in the potential reproduces a known expression once this is written out order by order in potential. The difficulty here is similar to that in Section
C.4
, namely that it is fairly easy to expand the known expression for the stationary density
without
the normalisation. Working in Fourier-space, we will once again write both the expansion and the normalisation in modes.

The first step is to characterise the expansion terms
F
and to bring them in a form that lends itself more naturally to a comparison to the field theory. This characterisation involves some rather involved manipulations.
In a second step, we will then demonstrate that the resulting expansion agrees with the diagrammatic one.

The classical stationary solution of the Fokker-Planck Eq. (
145
)
of a drift-diffusion particle
in one spatial dimension on a periodic domain and subject to a periodic potential defined so that

\Upsilon(x+L)=\Upsilon(x)
for
x\in[0,L)
is
[
23
,
22
]
,

\rho_{0}(x)=\mathcal{N}^{-1}Z_{-}(x)\int_{x}^{x+L}\!\!\!\!\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}Z_{+}(y)\quad\text{ with }\quad Z_{\pm}(x)=\operatorname{exp}\left(\pm\frac{\Upsilon(x)-w_{0}x}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)\ ,

(193)

similar to Eq. (
169
) defined with an explicit normalisation,

\mathcal{N}=\int_{0}^{L}\!\!\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}x\,}{\!\mathrm{d}x\,}{\!\mathrm{d}x\,}{\!\mathrm{d}x\,}Z_{-}(x)\int_{x}^{x+L}\!\!\!\!\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}Z_{+}(y)\ .

(194)

The density

\rho_{0}(x)=\mathcal{N}^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{L}\sum_{n=-\infty}^{\infty}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x\right)}F_{j}(k_{n})

(195)

can be written systematically in orders of the potential up to the normalisation to be determined below.
If
F_{j}(k_{n})
is the term of order
j
in
\Upsilon
, expanding
Z_{\pm}(x)=\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{\mp w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mp w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mp w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(\mp w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}\sum_{n}(\pm\Upsilon(x)/\mathsf{D}_{t})^{n}/n!
in Eq. (
193
), immediately gives

F_{j}(k_{n})=\sum_{i=0}^{j}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\frac{1}{(j-i)!}f_{j,i}(k_{n})\quad\text{ with }\quad f_{j,i}(k_{n})=\int_{0}^{L}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}x\,}{\!\mathrm{d}x\,}{\!\mathrm{d}x\,}{\!\mathrm{d}x\,}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x\right)}\left(\frac{\Upsilon(x)}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{i}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}\int_{x}^{x+L}\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}\left(\frac{\Upsilon(y)}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{j-i}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}y/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}y/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}y/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}y/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}\ .

(196)

An easy route to perform the Fourier transformation on the right is to split it into two and perform separate Fourier transformations of
(-\Upsilon(x)/\mathsf{D}_{t})^{i}
and of the second term,
\int_{x}^{x+L}\!\!\mathchoice{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}{\!\mathrm{d}y\,}\dots
tying both together again by a convolution afterwards. While the first term produces essentially Eq. (
173
), the second term involving the integral is more complicated. Writing however

\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x+w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x+w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x+w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x+w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}=\frac{1}{-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}\mathchoice{\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}}{\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x}{\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x}{\mathrm{d}/\mathrm{d}x}\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x+w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x+w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x+w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}x+w_{0}x/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}\ ,

(197)

which is well-defined as
-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}\neq 0
for all
n
by virtue of
k_{n}
and
w_{0}
being real and
w_{0}\neq 0
, allows the second integral to be removed by an integration by parts. Surface terms then drop out as
\Upsilon(x)
is periodic. Following at first the pattern of Eq. (
183
) the resulting Fourier series reads

f_{j,i}(k_{n})=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{n_{0}=-\infty}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{L\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{i}L\sum_{n_{1},\ldots,n_{i-1}}\Upsilon_{n_{0}-n_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{n_{i-2}-n_{i-1}}\Upsilon_{n_{i-1}}\\
\times\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{-\mathring{\imath}k_{n-n_{0}}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}\left(\frac{1}{L\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)^{j-i}L\sum_{m_{1},\ldots,m_{j-i-1}}\Upsilon_{n-n_{0}-m_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{m_{j-i-2}-n_{j-i-1}}\Upsilon_{n_{j-i-1}}\ .

(198)

This expression has some shortcomings that are mostly down to notation, which can be seen in the first sum by setting
i=0
or in the second sum by setting
i=j
. In both cases, the sums are ill-defined, with the first sum running over indeces
n_{1},\ldots,n_{-1}
for
i=0
and similarly the last sum for
i=j
. Choosing
i=1
or
i=j-1
causes similar problems. However, Eq. (
196
) allows for all of these choices. The problem is resolved by changing the indeces similar to Eq. (
184
), first in the individual Fourier-transforms and then after tying them together in a convolution

f_{j,i}(k_{n})=\frac{1}{(L\mathsf{D}_{t})^{j}}\sum_{p_{1},\ldots,p_{j}}\Upsilon_{p_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{p_{j}}L\delta_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j},n}\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{-\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}

(199)

with the convention of
k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}=0
for
i=j
, as can be verified by direct evaluation of the corresponding Fourier transform.
Similarly, we demand
\delta_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j},n}=\delta_{0,n}
for
j=0
, so that
f_{0,0}(k_{n})=L\delta_{0,n}(1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)})\mathsf{D}_{t}/w_{0}
, confirmed by direct evaluation of Eq. (
196
).
We state in passing
F_{j}
for
j=0,1
by direct evaluation of Eqs. (
196
) and (
199
),

\displaystyle F_{0}(k_{n})

\displaystyle=L\delta_{0,n}\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}

(200a)

\displaystyle F_{1}(k_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathsf{D}_{t}}\Upsilon_{n}\left\{\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}-\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right\}

(200b)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathring{\imath}k_{n}\Upsilon_{n}}{w_{0}}\frac{1}{-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}\ .

(200c)

We proceed by deriving a recurrence formula for
F_{j}
.

C.5.1
Properties of
F_{j}(k_{n})

Eq. (
199
) is to be used to compile
F_{j}(k_{n})
according to Eq. (
196
),

F_{j}(k_{n})=\sum_{i=0}^{j}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\frac{1}{(j-i)!}\frac{1}{(L\mathsf{D}_{t})^{j}}\sum_{p_{1},\ldots,p_{j}}\Upsilon_{p_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{p_{j}}L\delta_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j},n}\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{-\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}

(201)

which we rewrite for
j\geq 1
as

F_{j}(k_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\frac{1}{(j-i)!}f_{j,i}(k_{n})-\sum_{i=1}^{j}{j\choose i}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{j!}f_{j,0}(k_{n})

(202)

where the second term is the term for
i=0
which has been removed from the first sum and multiplied by a clever unity for
j\geq 1
,

1=-\sum_{i=1}^{j}{j\choose i}(-1)^{i}

(203)

as
(1-1)^{j}=0
for
j\geq 1
, so that

F_{j}(k_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\frac{1}{(j-i)!}\frac{1}{(L\mathsf{D}_{t})^{j}}\sum_{p_{1},\ldots,p_{j}}\Upsilon_{p_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{p_{j}}L\delta_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j},n}\left[\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{-\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}-\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{-\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j}}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right]\ ,

(204)

using
{j\choose i}/j!=1/(i!(j-i)!)
.
The square bracket can be simplified further as
k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j}}=k_{n}
by the Kronecker
\delta
-function and

k_{n}-k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}=k_{p_{j-i+1}+\ldots+p_{j}}=k_{p_{j-i+1}}+k_{p_{j-i+2}}+\ldots+k_{p_{j}}

(205)

so that

F_{j}(k_{n})=\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\frac{1}{(j-i)!}\frac{1}{(L\mathsf{D}_{t})^{j}}\\
\times\sum_{p_{1},\ldots,p_{j}}\Upsilon_{p_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{p_{j}}L\delta_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j},n}\left[\frac{(1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)})(-\mathring{\imath}(k_{p_{j-i+1}}+k_{p_{j-i+2}}+\dots+k_{p_{j}}))}{(-\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})}\right]\ .

(206)

The last term in the numerator of the square bracket,
(-\mathring{\imath}(k_{p_{j-i+1}}+k_{p_{j-i+2}}+\dots+k_{p_{j}}))
, which is
(-\mathring{\imath})
times Eq. (
205
) can be split into
i
terms, resulting in
i
sums over
p_{1},\dots,p_{j}
for each
i
. But every sum is in fact equal, as the
p_{1},\ldots,p_{j}
are dummy variables, so that the sum involving, say
p_{j-i+1}
, in the numerator can be turned into the sum involving
p_{j}
, simply by swapping the dummy variables
p_{j-i+1}
and
p_{j}
and then noticing that all other terms, such as
\Upsilon_{p_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{p_{j}}
or
k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}
are invariant under such permutations. In fact, the indeces of the dummy variables divide naturally into two sets, namely
\{1,\ldots,j-i\}
as used in
k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}
in one of the factors in the denominator, at the complement,
\{j-i+1,\ldots,j\}=\{1,\ldots,j\}\setminus\{1,\ldots,j-i\}
.
In other words for
j\geq 1

\displaystyle F_{j}(k_{n})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\frac{1}{(j-i)!}\frac{1}{(L\mathsf{D}_{t})^{j}}\sum_{p_{1},\ldots,p_{j}}\Upsilon_{p_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{p_{j}}L\delta_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j},n}\left[\frac{i(1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)})(-\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{j}})}{(-\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})}\right]

(207a)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L\mathsf{D}_{t}}\sum_{p_{j}}\frac{\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{j}}}{(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})}\Upsilon_{p_{j}}\sum_{i=1}^{j}\frac{(-1)^{i-1}}{(i-1)!}\frac{1}{(j-i)!}\frac{1}{(L\mathsf{D}_{t})^{j-1}}

(207b)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{p_{1},\ldots,p_{j-1}}\Upsilon_{p_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{p_{j-1}}L\delta_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-1},n-p_{j}}\left[\frac{(1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)})}{(-\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-i}}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L\mathsf{D}_{t}}\sum_{p_{j}}\frac{\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{j}}}{(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})}\Upsilon_{p_{j}}\sum_{i=0}^{j-1}\frac{(-1)^{i}}{i!}\frac{1}{(j-1-i)!}\frac{1}{(L\mathsf{D}_{t})^{j-1}}

(207c)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sum_{p_{1},\ldots,p_{j-1}}\Upsilon_{p_{1}}\ldots\Upsilon_{p_{j-1}}L\delta_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-1},n-p_{j}}\left[\frac{(1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)})}{(-\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{1}+\ldots+p_{j-1-i}}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L\mathsf{D}_{t}}\sum_{p_{j}}\frac{\mathring{\imath}k_{p_{j}}}{(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})}\Upsilon_{p_{j}}F_{j-1}(k_{n}-k_{p_{j}})\ ,

(207d)

where we have used the equivalence of the
i
terms in Eq. (
206
) to write Eq. (
207a
) which carries a factor of
i
in the numerator in the square bracket, taken the summation over
p_{j}
and other terms outside the rest of the expression in Eq. (
207b
), shifted
i
by unity in Eq. (
207c
), to finally re-express the sum as a convolution of a pre-factor with
F_{j-1}(k_{n})
using Eq. (
201
). Eq. (
207d
) is the central result of thise section, providing a simple recurrence formula for
F_{j}(k_{n})
for all
j\geq 1
. We will use Eq. (
207d
) in the following section to demonstrate the equivalence of the classical result Eq. (
195
) and the field theoretic result Eq. (
157
) with Eq. (
156
) in the presence of drift in one dimension.

C.5.2
Relationship between
E_{j}(k_{n})
and
F_{j}(k_{n})

Similar to Section
C.4.2
, we will now demonstrate that the density
\rho_{0}
expanded in
F_{j}(k_{n})
according to Eq. (
195
) with recurrence Eq. (
207d
) and with normalisation Eq. (
194
), is equal to that expanded in
E_{j}(k_{n})
according to Eqs. (
157
) and, in one dimension, (
164
). We will do so by demonstrating that the
j
th order in
\Upsilon
of
\mathcal{N}\rho_{0}
with
\rho_{0}
according to Eq. (
157
) equals
F_{j}(k_{n})
. Expressing
\mathcal{N}
in terms of
F_{j}(k_{n})
from Eqs. (
194
) and (
195
), this amounts to the task of showing

\sum_{i=0}^{j}F_{i}(0)E_{j-i}(k_{n})=F_{j}(k_{n})\ .

(208)

similar to Eq. (
177
), but now allowing for
w_{0}\neq 0
and restricting ourselves to one dimension. Eq. (
208
) clearly holds for
j=0
, as

F_{0}(0)E_{0}(k_{n})=L\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}\delta_{n,0}=F_{0}(k_{n})

(209)

using Eqs. (
158
) and (
200a
). It also holds for the more complicated
j=1
, Eq. (
200b
),

F_{1}(0)E_{0}(k_{n})+F_{0}(0)E_{1}(k_{n})=\left(L\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}\right)\left(-\frac{1}{L}\frac{k_{n}\Upsilon_{n}\mathcal{I}(n\neq 0)}{\mathsf{D}_{t}k_{n}+\mathring{\imath}w_{0}}\right)=\frac{\mathring{\imath}k_{n}\Upsilon_{n}}{w_{0}}\frac{1-\mathchoice{\mathrm{e}^{-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}}}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}{\operatorname{exp}\left(-w_{0}L/\mathsf{D}_{t}\right)}}{-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t}}

(210)

using Eqs. (
159
) and (
200b
) at
k_{n}=0
, the former in one dimension and the latter producing

F_{1}(0)=0
. In the last equality of Eq. (
210
) we have dropped the indicator function as
k_{n}\mathcal{I}(n\neq 0)=k_{n}
, as the denominator has no zero at
k_{n}=0
given that
w_{0}\neq 0
.

As in Section
C.4.2
, we assume Eq. (
208
) to hold for
j
and use Eqs. (
164
) and (
207d
) to show
in the following that Eq. (
208
) holds for
j+1
. Writing therefore for
j\geq 0

\displaystyle F_{j+1}(k_{n})

\displaystyle=(\mathcal{I}(n\neq 0)+\delta_{n,0})F_{j+1}(k_{n})

(211a)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathcal{I}(n\neq 0)}{L\mathsf{D}_{t}}\sum_{q}\frac{\mathring{\imath}k_{n-q}}{(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})}\Upsilon_{n-q}F_{j}(k_{q})+E_{0}(k_{n})F_{j+1}(0)

(211b)

as in Eq. (
190
) using Eq. (
181
) in the first line and in the second line using Eq. (
207d
) with
p_{j}
replaced by
n-q
to rewrite
F_{j+1}(k_{n})
and
\delta_{\mathbf{n},\mathbf{0}}
replaced by
E_{0}(k_{n})
according to Eq. (
158
). The
F_{j}(k_{q})
can now be replaced by Eq. (
208
),

F_{j+1}(k_{n})=\frac{\mathcal{I}(n\neq 0)}{L\mathsf{D}_{t}}\sum_{q}\frac{\mathring{\imath}k_{n-q}}{(-\mathring{\imath}k_{n}+w_{0}/\mathsf{D}_{t})}\Upsilon_{n-q}\sum_{i=0}^{j}F_{i}(0)E_{j-i}(k_{q})+E_{0}(k_{n})F_{j+1}(0)

(212)

and the convolution performed on
E_{j}(k_{n})
, Eq. (
164
),
which gives

F_{j+1}(k_{n})=\sum_{i=0}^{j}F_{i}(0)E_{j-i+1}(k_{n})+E_{0}(k_{n})F_{j+1}(0)=\sum_{i=0}^{j+1}F_{i}(0)E_{j+1-i}(k_{n})

(213)

confirming Eq. (
208
) for
j+1
, provided it holds for
j
. With Eq. (
213
) as the induction step and Eqs. (
209
) or (
210
) as the base case, this confirms Eq. (
157
) with (
164
) in the case of drift diffusion in one dimension.

C.6
Summary

In the present appendix, we have introduced a general formula to determine the stationary density
\rho_{0}(\mathbf{x})
Eq. (
157
) digrammatically, recursively and perturbatively in the potential, Eq. (
163
). We have validated this expression for the case of pure diffusion producing the barometric formula, Eq. (
169
), in Section
C.4
and for the case of drift-diffusion in one dimension, Eq. (
193
), in Section
C.5
. In both cases, we have used induction to show the equivalence of the density derived from theory and the density derived from classical considerations. The key challenge is to compare the field theoretic terms to a certain order in the potential to the corresponding terms in the classical result, which is straight-forwardly expanded in powers of the potential only up to a normalisation, which needs to be expanded itself, Eqs. (
176
), (
177
) and (
208
).

In order to treat the potential perturbatively, we had to allow for vanishing potential, which allows for a stationary state only if the system is finite. However, the resulting perturbation theory reproducing the Boltzmann factor in the case of no drift means that it applies equally for
confining
potentials. This applies similarly to the case with drift, as the thermodynamic limit
L\to\infty
may be taken after resumming the perturbative result into the integral expression Eq. (
193
).
